\section{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{Forensic Intelligence Report:
{[}YGS-2026-02{]}}}{Forensic Intelligence Report: {[}YGS-2026-02{]}}}\label{forensic-intelligence-report-ygs-2026-02}

\textbf{Subject:} Analysis of Modular iApp-Lua Surveillance Variant
("Huji Assistant")

\textbf{Threat Category:} Modular Dropper / Identity Spyware

\textbf{Target Platform:} Android (ARMv8/ARMv7/x86)

\textbf{Security Level:} High-Priority / Forensic Investigation

\textbf{Reference Case:} Similar in architecture and behavior to
\textbf{DOI: 10.5281/zenodo.18320726}

\textbf{Author:} Apich Organization Security Team

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{1. Executive
Summary}}{1. Executive Summary}}\label{1-executive-summary}

Forensic analysis of sample \textbf{YGS-2026-02} (labeled: 戸 籍 开 道
助 手 .apk) reveals a highly modularized surveillance tool utilizing the
\textbf{iApp (ygsiyu)} framework. This variant represents a strategic
shift from the gaming-themed masquerade of \textbf{YGS-2026-01} toward a
government-themed social engineering lure. By mimicking a "Resident
Registration Assistant," the threat actor seeks to justify broad
permission requests to facilitate the exfiltration of sensitive
Personally Identifiable Information (PII).

The file structure of \textbf{YGS-2026-02} above reveals a
sophisticated, multi-engine payload architecture that combines
script-based agility with native-level system access. The presence of
the \textbf{BeanShell (\texttt{bsh})} directory is a critical discovery;
BeanShell is a Java source interpreter that allows the malware to
execute "scripted" Java code dynamically without needing to compile new
DEX files, providing a stealthy alternative to the Lua engine. This is
complemented by the \textbf{\texttt{rc4加解密.dex}} module, which
contains the \textbf{\texttt{ADRTLogCatReader}} Smali classes. This
confirms that the malware\textquotesingle s primary surveillance
mechanism is focused on "Logcat scraping"---capturing system-wide logs
to intercept sensitive data like one-time passwords (OTPs), private
tokens, and app-to-app communications that are often inadvertently
leaked to the Android log buffer.

Furthermore, the inventory of the \texttt{/lib} directory proves a
"nested payload" strategy designed to defeat simple static analysis. By
bundling secondary JAR files like \textbf{\texttt{yecian.jar}} and
\textbf{\texttt{CmZ6NzczSVRN.jar}} alongside specialized native
libraries, the threat actor has built a toolkit that is
framework-agnostic. The \texttt{smali\_lib} structure shows that even
the "cryptography" module is actually a container for the
\textbf{\texttt{example.application4}} namespace, a likely intentional
use of generic "boilerplate" naming to hide malicious intent within
seemingly harmless sample code. This tiered structure---Lua for logic,
BeanShell for dynamic Java execution, and ADRT for log
surveillance---marks this variant as a highly versatile and dangerous
evolution of the YGS malware family.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{2. Relationship to
YGS-2026-01}}{2. Relationship to YGS-2026-01}}\label{2-relationship-to-ygs-2026-01}

The two samples share a common genetic lineage, suggesting they
originate from the same development cluster in the Fujian/Wuhan region.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Architectural Parity:} Both samples utilize the
  \texttt{libygsiyu.so} engine and the \texttt{libluajava.so} bridge to
  execute high-level malicious logic via Lua scripts.
\item
  \textbf{Common Evasion Tactics:} Both employ the
  \textbf{"Library-as-Payload"} technique, concealing non-native Java
  bytecode (\texttt{.jar}, \texttt{.dex}) within the \texttt{/lib}
  directory to bypass standard static analysis engines that prioritize
  scanning the \texttt{assets/} and root folders.
\item
  \textbf{Infrastructure Synergy:} Initial indicators suggest a shared
  Command \& Control (C2) backend, utilizing the same tiered
  Windows/IIS/MySQL stack found in the previous investigation.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{3. Payload Inventory \& Component
Analysis}}{3. Payload Inventory \& Component Analysis}}\label{3-payload-inventory--component-analysis}

The \texttt{/lib} directory of YGS-2026-02 contains several specialized
modules not found in the initial variant, indicating an upgrade in
cryptographic capabilities.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Component & File Type & Functional Classification & Forensic
Significance \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{rc4加解密.dex} & Dalvik Executable & \textbf{Crypto Engine} &
Dedicated RC4 module for decrypting secondary payloads and obfuscating
C2 traffic. \\
\texttt{yecian.jar} & Java Archive & \textbf{Exfiltration Manager} &
Orchestrates the collection and transmission of device metadata and user
logs. \\
\texttt{CmZ6NzczSVRN.jar} & Java Archive & \textbf{Surveillance Plugin}
& Likely handles specific identity-theft hooks related to the "Huji"
lure. \\
\texttt{sign.jar} & Java Archive & \textbf{Integrity Checker} &
Facilitates self-updates and verifies the signature of dynamically
loaded modules. \\
\end{longtable}

Analysis of the trojanized assets \texttt{assets/res/gx.mp3} and
\texttt{assets/res/.ant.dat} reveals that the malware is not a static
payload but an \textbf{active IDE-Worm}. These files contain \textbf{Yu
Code} (iApp scripting language) rather than media data, designed to
infect the victim developer\textquotesingle s local environment and any
future projects they create.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{Infection \& Persistence
Mechanism}}{Infection \& Persistence Mechanism}}\label{infection--persistence-mechanism}

The malware executes a recursive search across the
device\textquotesingle s external storage specifically targeting the
directory structure of the \textbf{iApp Development Environment}
(\texttt{\%iApp/ProjectApp/}).

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Project Hijacking:} The script identifies the entry point of
  other iApp projects (\texttt{mian.iyu}) and injects malicious hooks
  into the \texttt{loading} event.
\item
  \textbf{Worm Propagation:} It copies itself (\texttt{gx.mp3}) into the
  \texttt{/res} folder of the victim\textquotesingle s own projects.
\item
  \textbf{Resultant Impact:} Any application compiled by the developer
  on the infected device will inherently contain the YGS-2026-02
  surveillance logic, facilitating a \textbf{Supply Chain Attack}
  against the developer\textquotesingle s entire user base.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{Dynamic C2 Fallback
(DGA-Lite)}}{Dynamic C2 Fallback (DGA-Lite)}}\label{dynamic-c2-fallback-dga-lite}

The \texttt{.ant.dat} component acts as a \textbf{Resilience Module}. It
is programmed to scrape a public "Jimdo" blog page
(\texttt{antstudio.jimdofree{[}.{]}com}) to retrieve new Command \&
Control (C2) instructions.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Variable (Encoded) & Decoded Value & Functional Purpose \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{c} (btoo) & \texttt{http://docq.cn/files/edk88ke} & Primary C2
Payload Drop-point \\
\texttt{sss.bm} & \texttt{com.zhendong} & Targeted Package/Namespace \\
\texttt{ur} (reconstructed) &
\texttt{https://antstudio.jimdofree.com/test/virus} & Dead-Drop Resolver
for C2 migration \\
\end{longtable}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{Threat Actor Attribution
Indicators}}{Threat Actor Attribution Indicators}}\label{threat-actor-attribution-indicators}

The naming conventions (\texttt{antstu}, \texttt{ygsiyu}) and the use of
Jimdo as a dead-drop resolver are consistent with "Ant Studio"
development clusters. This group specializes in creating "Constructor"
kits that automate the creation of malware, allowing low-skill actors to
deploy high-impact surveillance tools.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{4. Security Impact \& Risk
Assessment}}{4. Security Impact \& Risk Assessment}}\label{4-security-impact--risk-assessment}

The discovery of YGS-2026-02 confirms that the threat actor is running a
concurrent, multi-vector campaign. The use of a dedicated \textbf{RC4
cryptographic module} represents a critical escalation, significantly
increasing the risk of \textbf{dark-attack vectors} and
\textbf{unauthorized data disclosure}.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Data Breach Risk:} High probability of mass exfiltration of
  resident identity data.
\item
  \textbf{Persistence:} The modular nature allows the attacker to swap
  lures (e.g., from PUBG tools to Government tools) while maintaining
  the same malicious backbone.
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{5. Technical
Appendix}}{5. Technical Appendix}}\label{5-technical-appendix}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Original Filename:} \texttt{戸\ 籍\ 开\ 道\ 助\ 手\ .apk}
\item
  \textbf{SHA-256 Hash:}
  \texttt{e90e20470814090d4d5c697d1860d76c412ea649978c6fa09d3b86de52d9d2cf}
\item
  \textbf{MD-5 Hash:} \texttt{c32cdda5d3aea27f0a8a2c5aeb2cf452}
\item
  \textbf{SHA-1 Hash:} \texttt{75a86ea825faffb6a96c5ce3d277c5c822568e9d}
\item
  \textbf{Recieve Method:} User Report
\item
  \textbf{Link:}
  \texttt{https://www.virustotal.com/gui/file/e90e20470814090d4d5c697d1860d76c412ea649978c6fa09d3b86de52d9d2cf/detection}
\item
  \textbf{Behavioral Results:} The behavioral results below reveal an
  aggressive and highly repetitive file-system propagation and
  exfiltration staging pattern typical of iApp-based malware. The
  application systematically iterates through nearly every standard
  Android media and system directory (DCIM, Download, Movies, Pictures,
  etc.) to write a functional iApp source file (mian.iyu) and an MP3
  asset (gx.mp3), suggesting a worm-like self-replication or a mechanism
  to ensure persistence even if the main APK is removed. The presence of
  libygsiyu.so and libluajava.so in memory confirms the active use of
  the iApp engine to orchestrate these actions.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Network:} On the network front, the malware establishes a
  clear command-and-control (C2) link to docq.cn (47.89.9.97) via port
  80, while simultaneously utilizing TLS-encrypted traffic (likely for
  data exfiltration) to Cloudflare-backed IPs. The specific JA3
  fingerprints and the SNI for \url{www.googleapis.com} indicate an
  attempt to blend in with legitimate Google background services to
  evade network-level detection. The deletion of userencryption.xml is
  particularly concerning, as it suggests the malware may be actively
  tampering with device encryption settings or clearing its own traces
  after a successful payload delivery, pointing to a high-risk
  compromise of the device\textquotesingle s security integrity.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Process:} The process execution and service activity logs
  confirm that this sample is a high-privilege escalation threat
  designed for total device takeover. The execution of the su
  (SuperUser) command indicates an immediate attempt to gain root
  access, allowing the malware to bypass all Android sandbox
  restrictions. By hooking into the Accessibility Service, the app gains
  the ability to intercept keystrokes, read on-screen content (such as
  2FA codes), and simulate user touches, while the activation of the
  Audio Service suggests background eavesdropping or call recording
  capabilities. The start of the com.iapp.app.run.mian activity with the
  specific intent parameter \{"key": "OpenFilexmlui", "value":
  "zyj.iyu"\} is the "smoking gun" for the execution of its primary
  malicious logic. This indicates that the iApp engine is loading a
  secondary, likely encrypted, UI/script file named zyj.iyu. Combined
  with the masquerade as a Google Games Service, this behavioral profile
  depicts a sophisticated Trojan that leverages administrative
  privileges and accessibility hooks to maintain persistence and conduct
  silent surveillance under the guise of a legitimate system process.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Process and service actions}
\NormalTok{Shell commands}

\NormalTok{    su}

\NormalTok{Services opened}

\NormalTok{    com.google.android.gms.games.service.GamesIntentService (com.google.android.gms)}
\NormalTok{    accessibility}

\NormalTok{Activities Started}

\NormalTok{    com.bblcx186946884582/com.iapp.app.run.mian {-} [\{"key": "OpenFilexmlui", "value": "zyj.iyu"\}]}

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Runtime:} The runtime telemetry confirms that the
  malware\textquotesingle s execution core is built on a highly dynamic
  Lua-Java bridge, which it uses to manipulate system-level behavior
  while evading static signature detection. By loading the luajava and
  ygsiyu (iApp) modules into memory, the application establishes a
  runtime environment capable of invoking native Android APIs directly
  from interpreted scripts. The invocation of
  android.os.SystemProperties.get indicates an active fingerprinting
  phase, where the malware retrieves sensitive device metadata---such as
  the IMEI, build version, or root status---to tailor its payload or
  detect analysis environments (anti-sandboxing). Simultaneously, the
  repeated calls to androidx.appcompat.app.AppCompatDialog and
  com.android.internal.policy.impl.PhoneWindow methods reveal a
  sophisticated UI overlay and hijacking strategy. By controlling dialog
  visibility (show, dismiss) and enforcing persistence
  (setCancelable(false), setCanceledOnTouchOutside(false)), the malware
  can create non-dismissible windows or "blocking" screens to facilitate
  social engineering or prevent user interference during a root-level
  attack. Furthermore, the inclusion of Conscrypt OpenSSL classes
  (OpenSSLCipher\$Mode and Padding) within the invoked methods suggests
  that the Lua script is actively managing its own encrypted
  communication channel, likely to wrap exfiltrated data in a secondary
  layer of encryption before it is sent to the docq.cn C2 server.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Crypto:} The observation of javax.crypto.Cipher.doFinal
  combined with the presence of multiple hardcoded 128-bit AES keys
  provides definitive proof of the malware\textquotesingle s
  cryptographic strategy for payload protection and data exfiltration.
  The use of the AES algorithm indicates a shift from the simple XOR
  "red herrings" found in the static analysis toward a robust,
  industry-standard encryption scheme managed at the Java runtime. These
  keys---specifically the sequences beginning with 32 E7 B3 and 41 B1
  D0---likely serve as the master keys for decrypting the secondary iApp
  scripts (zyj.iyu) or for wrapping exfiltrated PII before it is
  transmitted over the network. The diversity of the observed keys
  suggests a multi-layered encryption architecture: one key may be
  dedicated to local file-system persistence (such as the written gx.mp3
  or mian.iyu files), while others facilitate the secure handshake with
  the C2 infrastructure (docq.cn). By invoking doFinal directly, the
  malware completes the transformation of raw system data or intercepted
  Accessibility logs into encrypted blobs that are indistinguishable
  from legitimate HTTPS traffic. For forensic purposes, these extracted
  hex strings are the "Golden Keys"---they can be used to retroactively
  decrypt any local assets or intercepted packets, potentially revealing
  the plaintext configuration of the "Huji Assistant" campaign.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Environment Detection:} The systematic lookup of these
  specific system properties confirms a highly developed anti-analysis
  and environment-awareness suite. By querying qemu.hw.mainkeys and
  persist.sys.ui.hw, the malware is actively checking for "hardware"
  signatures that distinguish a physical device from an emulator or
  sandbox environment. The lookups for debug.layout and
  viewroot.profile\_rendering further suggest the malware monitors
  whether developer tools or layout inspectors are active, allowing it
  to "go dark" or execute benign dummy code if it detects a researcher's
  presence. This environmental fingerprinting is a tactical prerequisite
  for its more aggressive actions. By verifying the absence of a
  debugger through these system flags, the malware ensures it is safe to
  proceed with the su escalation and the deployment of its AES-encrypted
  payloads. In a forensic context, this confirms that YGS-2026-02 is not
  a generic script-kiddie tool, but a professional-grade surveillance
  variant designed to remain dormant in automated testing environments
  while successfully compromising the high-value personal data of
  real-world users.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{6.
Updates}}{6. Updates}}\label{6-updates}

\subsubsection{1. Technical Discovery: The "Hydra" Protection
Layer}\label{1-technical-discovery-the-hydra-protection-layer}

The malware uses a \textbf{Multi-Stage Native Unpacker} designed to
thwart static analysis and environment modification (like re-signing).

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Deceptive Assets:} The file \texttt{assets/lib.so} is not a
  Linux shared object. Our XOR analysis revealed a "Stage 1" 4-byte XOR
  key (\texttt{0xFE1C8F81}) that restores a \texttt{PK} (ZIP) header.
  However, this is a \textbf{decoy header}; the underlying data remains
  high-entropy, indicating a second layer of encryption.
\item
  \textbf{Native Crypto Engine:} The native library
  \texttt{libygsiyu.so} is the actual "brain." It bypasses Java-level
  APIs by using \textbf{JNI} to call low-level crypto functions. We
  located strings for \texttt{AES/CBC/PKCS5Padding},
  \texttt{SecretKeySpec}, and \texttt{IvParameterSpec} within the binary
  at offset \texttt{0x570a0}.
\item
  \textbf{Signature-Bound Decryption:} The native dump showed the
  library accessing \texttt{getPackageManager} and \texttt{signatures}.
  This implies the malware calculates the \textbf{MD5 of the
  developer\textquotesingle s original signing certificate}
  (\texttt{CERT.RSA}) to derive the AES decryption key for
  \texttt{lib.so}. This explains why standard keys and manual XOR
  attempts fail.
\end{itemize}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ Crypto.Cipher }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ AES}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ binascii}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ itertools}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Your observed 16{-}byte keys from the sandbox}
\NormalTok{KEYS\_HEX }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
    \StringTok{"32E7B35ADA13BEA8CF404851152AA011"}\NormalTok{,}
    \StringTok{"41B1D0B984BD880A5F707851FDCA6038"}\NormalTok{,}
    \StringTok{"501093F28411FE6264731BEC7A1E040E"}\NormalTok{,}
    \StringTok{"54D7230A2CBD299534BA7E2149F62023"}
\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{keys\_bin }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [binascii.unhexlify(k) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ KEYS\_HEX]}

\CommentTok{\# 2. The 21{-}byte static XOR key we found in the Ghidra \_\_s\_00 array}
\NormalTok{STATIC\_XOR\_KEY }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{([}
    \BaseNTok{0xE2}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x48}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x25}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xE7}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x80}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x46}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xE3}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x3C}\NormalTok{,}
    \BaseNTok{0x3C}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x1E}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x25}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x1D}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x4E}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x05}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x55}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xE1}\NormalTok{,}
    \BaseNTok{0x69}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xA8}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x18}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xCA}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x00}
\NormalTok{])}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ master\_decrypt(input\_file, output\_file):}
    \ControlFlowTok{with} \BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(input\_file, }\StringTok{"rb"}\NormalTok{) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ f:}
\NormalTok{        ciphertext }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f.read()}

    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[*] Loaded }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{input\_file}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(ciphertext)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ bytes)"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"[*] Testing all AES combinations and XOR layers..."}\NormalTok{)}

    \CommentTok{\# Try every combination of Key and IV (12 permutations total)}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k, iv }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ itertools.permutations(keys\_bin, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{):}
        \CommentTok{\# Step 1: AES Decrypt (CBC Mode)}
\NormalTok{        cipher }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(k, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}
        \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            aes\_decrypted }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{(cipher.decrypt(ciphertext))}
        \ControlFlowTok{except} \PreprocessorTok{Exception}\NormalTok{:}
            \ControlFlowTok{continue}

        \CommentTok{\# Step 2: Apply the 21{-}byte Rolling XOR}
        \CommentTok{\# We do this BEFORE the slky brute force because Ghidra showed}
        \CommentTok{\# the rolling XOR loop occurring in the decryption flow.}
\NormalTok{        xor\_layer\_data }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(aes\_decrypted))}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(aes\_decrypted)):}
\NormalTok{            xor\_layer\_data[i] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ aes\_decrypted[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ STATIC\_XOR\_KEY[i }\OperatorTok{\%} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(STATIC\_XOR\_KEY)]}

        \CommentTok{\# Step 3: Brute force the single \textquotesingle{}slky\textquotesingle{} byte (0{-}255)}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ slky\_byte }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{256}\NormalTok{):}
            \CommentTok{\# Check the first 4 bytes for magic headers}
\NormalTok{            header }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{([xor\_layer\_data[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ slky\_byte }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)])}

            \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ header.startswith(}\StringTok{b"PK"}\NormalTok{) }\KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ header.startswith(}\StringTok{b"dex"}\NormalTok{):}
                \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{[!] SUCCESS! FOUND VALID PAYLOAD"}\NormalTok{)}
                \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] Header: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{header}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] AES Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{binascii}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{hexlify(k)}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{upper()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] AES IV:  }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{binascii}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{hexlify(iv)}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{upper()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] Slky XOR Byte: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{hex}\NormalTok{(slky\_byte)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

                \CommentTok{\# Apply final XOR and save}
\NormalTok{                final\_data }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{([b }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ slky\_byte }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ b }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ xor\_layer\_data])}
                \ControlFlowTok{with} \BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(output\_file, }\StringTok{"wb"}\NormalTok{) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ out:}
\NormalTok{                    out.write(final\_data)}
                \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] Decrypted file saved as: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{output\_file}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{True}

    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{[{-}] Decryption failed. Possible reasons:"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"    1. The lib.so file has extra padding/headers."}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"    2. The 21{-}byte XOR happens in a different order."}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{False}

\CommentTok{\# Run it}
\ControlFlowTok{if} \VariableTok{\_\_name\_\_} \OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{"\_\_main\_\_"}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{    master\_decrypt(}\StringTok{"./assets/lib.so"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"decrypted\_payload.bin"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

    \BaseNTok{0x69}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xA8}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x18}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xCA}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x00}
\NormalTok{])}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ deep\_scan(input\_file):}
    \ControlFlowTok{with} \BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(input\_file, }\StringTok{"rb"}\NormalTok{) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ f:}
\NormalTok{        full\_data }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f.read()}

    \CommentTok{\# Try different starting offsets in case there is a header}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ offset }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{        ciphertext }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ full\_data[offset:]}
        \CommentTok{\# Ensure ciphertext is multiple of 16 for AES}
\NormalTok{        mod }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(ciphertext) }\OperatorTok{\%} \DecValTok{16}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ mod }\OperatorTok{!=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            ciphertext }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ciphertext[:}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{mod]}

        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[*] Testing Offset }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{offset}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{..."}\NormalTok{)}

        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k, iv }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ itertools.permutations(keys\_bin, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{            cipher }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(k, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}
            \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{                raw\_aes }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{(cipher.decrypt(ciphertext))}
            \ControlFlowTok{except}\NormalTok{:}
                \ControlFlowTok{continue}

            \CommentTok{\# Case A: AES {-}\textgreater{} Rolling XOR {-}\textgreater{} Slky}
            \CommentTok{\# Case B: AES {-}\textgreater{} Slky (No Rolling)}
            \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ mode }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}rolling\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}no\_rolling\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{                current\_data }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{(raw\_aes)}
                \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ mode }\OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}rolling\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{:}
                    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(current\_data)):}
\NormalTok{                        current\_data[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}=}\NormalTok{ STATIC\_XOR\_KEY[i }\OperatorTok{\%} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(STATIC\_XOR\_KEY)]}

                \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ slky }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{256}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{                    h }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{([current\_data[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ slky }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)])}
                    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ h.startswith(}\StringTok{b"PK"}\NormalTok{) }\KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ h.startswith(}\StringTok{b"dex"}\NormalTok{):}
                        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{[!] SUCCESS at Offset }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{offset}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{!"}\NormalTok{)}
                        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] Mode: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{mode}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{binascii}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{hexlify(k)}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{upper()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[+] Slky Byte: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{hex}\NormalTok{(slky)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                        \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{True}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{False}

\NormalTok{deep\_scan(}\StringTok{"./assets/lib.so"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}

\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ hashlib}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ Crypto.Cipher }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ AES}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ os}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ sys}

\NormalTok{LIB\_SO\_PATH }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"lib.so"}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Key used inside slky() scrambling loop (local\_38)}
\CommentTok{\# Extracted from code2026{-}02{-}001.c (Lines 969+)}
\NormalTok{STATIC\_KEY\_SLKY }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{([}
    \BaseNTok{0xe2}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x5f}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x48}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x73}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x25}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xc6}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xe7}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x11}\NormalTok{, }
    \BaseNTok{0x80}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x7c}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x46}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xc3}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xe3}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x1d}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x3c}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x97}\NormalTok{, }
    \BaseNTok{0x3c}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x77}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x1e}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x01}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x00}
\NormalTok{])}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Key used in burden() rolling XOR (\_\_s\_00)}
\CommentTok{\# Reconstructed from code2026{-}02{-}001.c (Lines 678+)}
\NormalTok{STATIC\_KEY\_BURDEN }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{([}
    \BaseNTok{0xE2}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x48}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x25}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xE7}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x80}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x46}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xE3}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x3C}\NormalTok{,}
    \BaseNTok{0x3C}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x1E}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x25}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x1D}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x4E}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x05}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x55}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xE1}\NormalTok{,}
    \BaseNTok{0x69}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xA8}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x18}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0xCA}\NormalTok{, }\BaseNTok{0x00}
\NormalTok{])}

\NormalTok{KEYS\_HEX }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
    \StringTok{"32E7B35ADA13BEA8CF404851152AA011"}\NormalTok{,}
    \StringTok{"41B1D0B984BD880A5F707851FDCA6038"}\NormalTok{,}
    \StringTok{"501093F28411FE6264731BEC7A1E040E"}\NormalTok{,}
    \StringTok{"54D7230A2CBD299534BA7E2149F62023"}
\NormalTok{]}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ slky\_scramble(p1, p2, algo):}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ p1 }\KeywordTok{is} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{: p1 }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{b""}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ p2 }\KeywordTok{is} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{: p2 }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{b""}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Checksum logic matching C implementation}
\NormalTok{    sum\_val }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(p1)}
\NormalTok{    len\_p1 }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(p1) }\ControlFlowTok{if} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(p1) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{0} \ControlFlowTok{else} \DecValTok{1}
\NormalTok{    local\_64 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (sum\_val }\OperatorTok{\%}\NormalTok{ len\_p1)}
\NormalTok{    local\_64 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (local\_64 }\OperatorTok{+} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(p2)) }\OperatorTok{\&} \BaseNTok{0xFF}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ algo }\OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}md5\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: h }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ hashlib.md5()}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ algo }\OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}sha1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: h }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ hashlib.sha1()}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ algo }\OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}sha256\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: h }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ hashlib.sha256()}
    \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{b""}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Simulating MessageDigest.update(p1+p2)}
\NormalTok{    h.update(p1 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ p2)}
\NormalTok{    digest }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{(h.digest())}
\NormalTok{    len\_d }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(digest)}
    
\NormalTok{    local\_68 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (local\_64 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ (len\_d }\OperatorTok{//} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)) }\OperatorTok{\&} \BaseNTok{0xFF}
\NormalTok{    len\_k }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(STATIC\_KEY\_SLKY)}
\NormalTok{    k\_idx }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Scramble loop}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(len\_d):}
\NormalTok{        b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ digest[i]}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Consistent signed byte to int conversion}
\NormalTok{        val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ val }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{127}\NormalTok{: val }\OperatorTok{{-}=} \DecValTok{256}
\NormalTok{        abs\_val }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{abs}\NormalTok{(val)}
\NormalTok{        swap\_idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ abs\_val }\OperatorTok{\%}\NormalTok{ len\_d}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Conditional XOR before swap}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (len\_d }\OperatorTok{//} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{ swap\_idx:}
\NormalTok{            b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ local\_68}
            
        \CommentTok{\# Swap logic}
\NormalTok{        b\_at\_swap }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ digest[swap\_idx]}
\NormalTok{        digest[swap\_idx] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{\&} \BaseNTok{0xFF}
\NormalTok{        digest[i] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b\_at\_swap}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Final XOR with Static Key}
\NormalTok{        digest[i] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ digest[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ STATIC\_KEY\_SLKY[k\_idx]}
\NormalTok{        k\_idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (k\_idx }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{\%}\NormalTok{ len\_k}
        
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{(digest)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(data, key):}
\NormalTok{    res }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{(data)}
\NormalTok{    kl }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(key)}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(res)):}
\NormalTok{        res[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}=}\NormalTok{ key[i }\OperatorTok{\%}\NormalTok{ kl]}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{(res)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ check\_header(data):}
    \CommentTok{\# Magic Header Checks}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(data) }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{False}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ data.startswith(}\StringTok{b"PK}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}x03\textbackslash{}x04}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{"ZIP"}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ data.startswith(}\StringTok{b"dex}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{"DEX"}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ data.startswith(}\StringTok{b"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}x7f}\StringTok{ELF"}\NormalTok{): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{"ELF"}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{False}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ decrypt():}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Starting Decryption..."}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ os.path.exists(LIB\_SO\_PATH): }
        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Error: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{LIB\_SO\_PATH}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ not found"}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}
        
    \ControlFlowTok{with} \BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(LIB\_SO\_PATH, }\StringTok{"rb"}\NormalTok{) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ f:}
\NormalTok{        full\_data }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f.read()}

    \CommentTok{\# Prepare inputs for slky}
\NormalTok{    inputs\_bin }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{.fromhex(k) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ KEYS\_HEX]}
\NormalTok{    inputs\_txt }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [k.encode(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}utf{-}8\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ KEYS\_HEX]}
\NormalTok{    all\_inputs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ inputs\_bin }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ inputs\_txt}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Pre{-}calculate Dynamic Keys (Final XOR Layer)}
\NormalTok{    dyn\_keys }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
\NormalTok{    dyn\_keys.append((}\StringTok{b""}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"None"}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"[*] Generating Dynamic XOR Keys..."}\NormalTok{)}
    \CommentTok{\# MD5 is most likely given "Observed AES{-}128 Keys" context}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ algo }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}md5\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}sha1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]:}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k1 }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ all\_inputs:}
             \CommentTok{\# Try permutations of key pairs}
            \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k2 }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ all\_inputs:}
\NormalTok{                k }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ slky\_scramble(k1, k2, algo)}
\NormalTok{                dyn\_keys.append((k, }\SpecialStringTok{f"slky(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{algo}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{,pair)"}\NormalTok{))}
            \CommentTok{\# Try single key}
\NormalTok{            k }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ slky\_scramble(k1, }\StringTok{b""}\NormalTok{, algo)}
\NormalTok{            dyn\_keys.append((k, }\SpecialStringTok{f"slky(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{algo}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{,single)"}\NormalTok{))}
            
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[*] Generated }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(dyn\_keys)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ potential final layers."}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{    aes\_keys }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ inputs\_bin}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Test common file offsets}
\NormalTok{    skips }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{32}\NormalTok{]}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ skip }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ skips:}
        \CommentTok{\# Optimization: Process only header chunk first}
\NormalTok{        chunk }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ full\_data[skip:skip}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{64}\NormalTok{]}
        
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ aes\_k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ aes\_keys:}
            \CommentTok{\# IV is usually Zero or the Key itself in malware}
\NormalTok{            ivs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{b\textquotesingle{}}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}x00}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, aes\_k]}
            
            \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ iv }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ ivs:}
                \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
                    \CommentTok{\# Layer 1: AES Decrypt}
\NormalTok{                    cipher }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(aes\_k, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}
\NormalTok{                    pt\_aes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cipher.decrypt(chunk)}
                    
                    \CommentTok{\# Layer 2: Rolling XOR (Static)}
\NormalTok{                    pt\_static }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(pt\_aes, STATIC\_KEY\_BURDEN)}
                    
                    \CommentTok{\# Layer 3: Final XOR (Dynamic)}
                    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ dyn\_k, desc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ dyn\_keys:}
\NormalTok{                        final }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(pt\_static, dyn\_k)}
                        
\NormalTok{                        res }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ check\_header(final)}
                        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ res:}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{[+] SUCCESS! Found }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{res}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ Header"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    Skip: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{skip}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    AES Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{aes\_k}\SpecialCharTok{.}\BuiltInTok{hex}\NormalTok{()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    IV: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{iv}\SpecialCharTok{.}\BuiltInTok{hex}\NormalTok{()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    Dynamic Key Desc: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{desc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            
                            \CommentTok{\# Perform full decryption}
\NormalTok{                            fc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(aes\_k, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}
\NormalTok{                            full\_pt }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fc.decrypt(full\_data[skip:])}
\NormalTok{                            full\_static }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(full\_pt, STATIC\_KEY\_BURDEN)}
\NormalTok{                            full\_final }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(full\_static, dyn\_k)}
                            
\NormalTok{                            out\_name }\OperatorTok{=} \SpecialStringTok{f"decrypted\_payload.}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{res}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{lower()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}
                            \ControlFlowTok{with} \BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(out\_name, }\StringTok{"wb"}\NormalTok{) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ f: f.write(full\_final)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    Saved payload to }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{out\_name}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \ControlFlowTok{return}
                \ControlFlowTok{except} \PreprocessorTok{Exception}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{pass}

    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"[{-}] Decryption failed with current permutations."}\NormalTok{)}

\ControlFlowTok{if} \VariableTok{\_\_name\_\_} \OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{"\_\_main\_\_"}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{    decrypt()}
\end{Highlighting}

\NormalTok{        b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ digest[i]}

\NormalTok{        val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b}

\NormalTok{            b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ local\_68}
            
        \CommentTok{\# Swap logic}
\NormalTok{        b\_at\_swap }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ digest[swap\_idx]}
\NormalTok{        digest[swap\_idx] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{\&} \BaseNTok{0xFF}
\NormalTok{        digest[i] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b\_at\_swap}

\NormalTok{        digest[i] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ digest[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ STATIC\_KEY\_SLKY[k\_idx]}
\NormalTok{        k\_idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (k\_idx }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{\%}\NormalTok{ len\_k}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(data, key):}

\NormalTok{    kl }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(key)}

\NormalTok{        res[i] }\OperatorTok{\^{}=}\NormalTok{ key[i }\OperatorTok{\%}\NormalTok{ kl]}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{(res)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ check\_header(data):}
    \CommentTok{\# Magic Header Checks}

    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ data.startswith(}\StringTok{b"PK}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}x03\textbackslash{}x04}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{"ZIP"}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ data.startswith(}\StringTok{b"dex}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{"DEX"}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ data.startswith(}\StringTok{b"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}x7f}\StringTok{ELF"}\NormalTok{): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \StringTok{"ELF"}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ decrypt():}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Starting Decryption..."}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ os.path.exists(LIB\_SO\_PATH): }

        \ControlFlowTok{return}

\NormalTok{        full\_data }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f.read()}

    \CommentTok{\# Prepare inputs for slky}
\NormalTok{    inputs\_bin }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\BuiltInTok{bytes}\NormalTok{.fromhex(k) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ KEYS\_HEX]}
\NormalTok{    inputs\_txt }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [k.encode(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}utf{-}8\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ KEYS\_HEX]}
\NormalTok{    all\_inputs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ inputs\_bin }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ inputs\_txt}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Pre{-}calculate Dynamic Keys (Final XOR Layer)}
\NormalTok{    dyn\_keys }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
\NormalTok{    dyn\_keys.append((}\StringTok{b""}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"None"}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"[*] Generating Dynamic XOR Keys..."}\NormalTok{)}

        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k1 }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ all\_inputs:}
             \CommentTok{\# Try permutations of key pairs}

\NormalTok{                k }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ slky\_scramble(k1, k2, algo)}
\NormalTok{                dyn\_keys.append((k, }\SpecialStringTok{f"slky(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{algo}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{,pair)"}\NormalTok{))}
            \CommentTok{\# Try single key}
\NormalTok{            k }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ slky\_scramble(k1, }\StringTok{b""}\NormalTok{, algo)}
\NormalTok{            dyn\_keys.append((k, }\SpecialStringTok{f"slky(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{algo}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{,single)"}\NormalTok{))}
            
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"[*] Generated }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(dyn\_keys)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ potential final layers."}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{    aes\_keys }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ inputs\_bin}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Test common file offsets}
\NormalTok{    skips }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{32}\NormalTok{]}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ skip }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ skips:}

\NormalTok{        chunk }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ full\_data[skip:skip}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{64}\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{            ivs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{b\textquotesingle{}}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}x00}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, aes\_k]}

\NormalTok{                        final }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(pt\_static, dyn\_k)}
                        
\NormalTok{                        res }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ check\_header(final)}
                        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ res:}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{[+] SUCCESS! Found }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{res}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ Header"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    Skip: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{skip}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    AES Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{aes\_k}\SpecialCharTok{.}\BuiltInTok{hex}\NormalTok{()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    IV: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{iv}\SpecialCharTok{.}\BuiltInTok{hex}\NormalTok{()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    Dynamic Key Desc: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{desc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            
                            \CommentTok{\# Perform full decryption}
\NormalTok{                            fc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(aes\_k, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}
\NormalTok{                            full\_pt }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fc.decrypt(full\_data[skip:])}
\NormalTok{                            full\_static }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(full\_pt, STATIC\_KEY\_BURDEN)}
\NormalTok{                            full\_final }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(full\_static, dyn\_k)}
                            
\NormalTok{                            out\_name }\OperatorTok{=} \SpecialStringTok{f"decrypted\_payload.}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{res}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{lower()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}
                            \ControlFlowTok{with} \BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(out\_name, }\StringTok{"wb"}\NormalTok{) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ f: f.write(full\_final)}
                            \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"    Saved payload to }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{out\_name}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                            \ControlFlowTok{return}
                \ControlFlowTok{except} \PreprocessorTok{Exception}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{pass}

    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"[{-}] Decryption failed with current permutations."}\NormalTok{)}

\ControlFlowTok{if} \VariableTok{\_\_name\_\_} \OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{"\_\_main\_\_"}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{    decrypt()}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ c\_mod(a, b):}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ decrypt():}

\NormalTok{    keys\_bin }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [binascii.unhexlify(k) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ k }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ KEYS\_HEX]}

\NormalTok{                    data\_xor }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(chunk, xor\_key)}
\NormalTok{                    cipher }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(dk, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}
\NormalTok{                    pt }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cipher.decrypt(data\_xor)}

Flow: XOR(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{xor\_key }\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{ dk }\KeywordTok{and} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}DK\textquotesingle{}} \KeywordTok{or} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}BURDEN\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) {-}\textgreater{} AES\_CBC. Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{desc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, IV: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}DK\textquotesingle{}} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ iv}\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{dk }\ControlFlowTok{else} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}0\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                        \ControlFlowTok{return}

\NormalTok{                    cipher }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ AES.new(dk, AES.MODE\_CBC, iv)}

\NormalTok{                    final }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ xor\_data(pt\_aes, xor\_key)}
                    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ check\_header(final):}
                        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"FOUND! Flow: AES\_CBC {-}\textgreater{} XOR(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{xor\_key }\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{ dk }\KeywordTok{and} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}DK\textquotesingle{}} \KeywordTok{or} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}BURDEN\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{). Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{desc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, IV: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}DK\textquotesingle{}} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ iv}\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{dk }\ControlFlowTok{else} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}0\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
                        \ControlFlowTok{return}

Key: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{desc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, IV: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}DK\textquotesingle{}} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ iv}\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{dk }\ControlFlowTok{else} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}0\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

Due to the time-sensitive nature of the investigation, the Apich
Organization Security Team has only partially decrypted the lib.so
payload. We are now calling upon the security community to assist in
completing the analysis.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{2. Structural Analysis: The iApp/Ant Studio
Framework}\label{2-structural-analysis-the-iappant-studio-framework}

The malware is built on the \textbf{iApp (Ant Studio)} development
framework, common in Chinese-origin grayware and Trojans.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Dynamic Loading:} The \texttt{logoActivity.smali} code
  confirms the app scans assets for a file named \texttt{yuv0.xml}. This
  file likely serves as a manifest for the "real" payload once the
  native layer decrypts it into memory.
\item
  \textbf{The "Yu Code" Interpreter:} We\textquotesingle ve identified
  \texttt{com.iapp.app.run} and classes like \texttt{Aid\_YuCodeX} as
  the execution engine. This engine runs scripts (likely \texttt{.iyu}
  or \texttt{.myu} files) that are hidden inside the encrypted
  \texttt{lib.so}. These scripts perform the actual malicious actions
  (SMS intercepting, C2 communication) while the Java layer remains
  "clean."
\item
  \textbf{Stateful Control:} The variable
  \texttt{Lc/b/a/a/w;-\textgreater{}o:I} acts as a global state flag.
  When set by the native library, it likely switches the engine into an
  "Encrypted Path," forcing the app to read its logic from a decrypted
  memory buffer rather than the standard filesystem.
\end{itemize}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ base64}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ re}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ get\_xor\_key():}
    \CommentTok{\# Replicating the Java: Base64Utils.encode("三生石畔 彼岸花开".getBytes())}
\NormalTok{    s }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ base64.b64encode(}\StringTok{"三生石畔 彼岸花开"}\NormalTok{.encode(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}utf{-}8\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)).decode(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}utf{-}8\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    \CommentTok{\# replaceAll("\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}D+", "") {-}\textgreater{} keep only numbers}
\NormalTok{    digits }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ re.sub(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{\textbackslash{}D}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, s)}
    \CommentTok{\# reverse and convert to int}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{int}\NormalTok{(digits[::}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{])}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ decrypt\_l(encrypted\_str):}
\NormalTok{    key }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ get\_xor\_key()}

    \CommentTok{\# 1. Reverse XOR with the dynamic key}
\NormalTok{    chars }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{(encrypted\_str)}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(chars)):}
\NormalTok{        chars[i] }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{chr}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{ord}\NormalTok{(chars[i]) }\OperatorTok{\^{}}\NormalTok{ key)}

    \CommentTok{\# 2. Base64 Decode}
\NormalTok{    b64\_decoded }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ base64.b64decode(}\StringTok{""}\NormalTok{.join(chars))}

    \CommentTok{\# 3. XOR with 255 (Bitwise NOT)}
\NormalTok{    final\_bytes }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{bytearray}\NormalTok{()}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ b }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ b64\_decoded:}
\NormalTok{        final\_bytes.append(b }\OperatorTok{\^{}} \DecValTok{255}\NormalTok{)}

    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ final\_bytes.decode(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}utf{-}8\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, errors}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}ignore\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Testing with the QQ group or other strings}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Dynamic Key Found: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{get\_xor\_key()}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ base64}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ re}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ sys}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ get\_xor\_key():}
    \CommentTok{\# Replicating Java: Base64Utils.encode("三生石畔 彼岸花开")}
    \CommentTok{\# Note: The Java code uses the specific Chinese characters as the seed}
\NormalTok{    seed }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"三生石畔 彼岸花开"}

\subsubsection{3. Technical Discovery: The "Ghost Server"
Backdoor}\label{3-technical-discovery-the-ghost-server-backdoor}

The malware utilizes an embedded web server to turn the infected device
into a remote-controlled terminal.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{BeanShell Remote Shell:} The discovery of
  \texttt{bsh/util/Httpd.smali} confirms the presence of a mini-web
  server. Unlike standard apps that "pull" data from a server, this app
  "listens" for incoming commands.
\item
  \textbf{Persistent Listening:} The
  \texttt{ServerSocket-\textgreater{}accept()} loop in
  \texttt{Httpd.smali} indicates that once the app is launched, it opens
  a port on the device. An attacker can "push" raw Java code or system
  commands directly to the phone via a simple HTTP POST request.
\item
  \textbf{Multi-Threaded Execution:} The
  \texttt{Ljava/lang/Thread;-\textgreater{}start()V} calls within the
  \texttt{Httpd} class allow the attacker to execute tasks (like file
  exfiltration or audio recording) in the background without affecting
  the user\textquotesingle s current activity.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{4. Decoded Indicators of Compromise
(IOCs)}\label{4-decoded-indicators-of-compromise-iocs}

Using the \texttt{btoo} (Byte-to-Object) decryption logic found in the
scripts, we have successfully unmasked the "plain text" values that the
malware uses to hide its operational hub.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Encoded Decimal & Decoded Value & Role in Malware \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{55\ 51\ 52\ 54\ 50\ 56\ 51\ 56\ 55} & \textbf{734628387} &
\textbf{The QQ Group ID.} This is the primary command and control (C2)
hub for the "Ant Studio" developers. \\
\texttt{-26\ -72\ -87\ ...} & \textbf{温馨提示} & "Warm Reminder" ---
Used as the title for fake system popups. \\
\texttt{-28\ -72\ -117\ ...} & \textbf{下载更新} & "Download Update" ---
The button text used to trick users into installing more malicious
stages. \\
\end{longtable}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{5. Defensive Infrastructure: The "China-Only"
Gatekeeper}\label{5-defensive-infrastructure-the-china-only-gatekeeper}

Our attempts to reach the command infrastructure revealed a high degree
of operational security (OPSEC).

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{IP Geofencing:} The \texttt{Connection\ reset\ by\ peer} and
  Cloudflare blocks suggest the attackers use "Regional Gatekeeping."
  The servers are configured to reject any traffic that does not
  originate from a specific range of Chinese mobile carrier IP
  addresses.
\item
  \textbf{Anti-Sandboxing:} The infrastructure is tuned to ignore
  \texttt{curl} or automated research tools, likely validating the
  \texttt{User-Agent} and device fingerprints before allowing the
  payload to download.
\end{itemize}
